Hello, Dr. Smith. It's a real pleasure to meet you, and thank you so much for seeing me today. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time.

Oh, my, well... I certainly wouldn't say I'm clumsy! I'm always very careful on my feet, I make sure of it. But... well, maybe just a little bit of trouble with my words sometimes. Nothing terrible, I promise! 

It's just... well, when the ladies ask me about things, like the charity blankets or choir practice, I really do mean to answer them straight. I try to be so polite and proper. But lately, my words just sort of run off on me. It makes me feel a bit silly, to be honest, having them stare at me like that. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish, Doctor! It's just a little conversational detour, really. Nothing to worry too much over, right? Am I explaining that okay for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. My friends haven't said anything like that to me. I'd be quite upset if they thought I was just... sitting there like a bump on a log! 

I've always been a very warm person, you know? I smile when I greet people, and I nod along when they tell me their stories. I mean, maybe sometimes I get a little tired and don't make a big fuss over every little thing, but I certainly have plenty of expression! And my blinking? Well, I can't say I've ever paid attention to how much I blink, but nobody has ever told me I'm staring at them. That would be terribly rude, wouldn't it? 

I always try to be polite and pleasant when I'm visiting. I hope that's what you were looking to hear? I really do try my best to be a good listener for my friends. Is that the sort of thing you needed to know?

Oh, my goodness, no. Nobody has ever said anything like that to me. I've always been quite spry on my feet, you know? 

I mean, I suppose I don't go running any marathons these days, and maybe I take my time a little more when I'm walking down to the mailbox, just to be careful not to trip. But a shuffle? Oh, absolutely not. I always make sure to pick my feet up properly. I'd be mortified if people thought I was shuffling around like some sort of... invalid! 

I try to keep myself looking nice and proper when I'm out and about. I hope that puts your mind at ease, Doctor? I really am doing wonderfully. Is that the kind of answer you were hoping for?

I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: During these moments, have you ever noticed any twitching or jerking movements in your hands, face, or anywhere else in your body?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor Smith, nothing like that at all. I hope it's what you need to hear to figure this all out. Am I being helpful enough, Doctor? I really am trying my best for you.\n\nDoctor: When you have these moments of drifting off, have you been feeling particularly overwhelmed, stressed, or worried about anything in your life lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no. I just putter around the house and mind my own business. \n\nI certainly wouldn't want to complain or make a fuss when there are folks out there with real problems, you know? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you are at home doing your usual chores, like cooking or keeping your house tidy, have you noticed that you are finding it harder to finish tasks or follow the steps required to complete them?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, heavens, no. I've been very blessed with my health, thank the Lord. I don't have any... well, any conditions like that at all. And I certainly don't take any of those strong prescription pills, either. 

I mean, I might take a little daily vitamin with my breakfast, just to be sensible, but I've always prided myself on taking proper care of my body. I eat my vegetables and keep a clean house, and I think that goes a long way, don't you? I'd just hate to be one of those people who is always at the doctor's office complaining about this ache or that pain. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone. 

So, no, I'm right as rain in that department. I hope that's good news for your paperwork, Doctor? It must be nice to have a healthy patient for a change, right?

Oh, dear me, no. I've never had a terrible fall or hit my head like that.

I don't rightly know why it happens, to be perfectly honest with you. I just sort of pause, I guess. Like my mind just wanders off for a spell and I just forget to keep going with what my hands are doing. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound too foolish. I'm trying my very best to explain it right for you, but it's just hard to put into words. Did I answer that the way you needed, Doctor? I can try to explain it better if I didn't do a good job.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you ever experience any unusual sensations, like a strange smell, a funny taste in your mouth, or a sudden feeling of déjà vu right before you stop moving?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, sir. Everything feels just perfectly normal right before it happens. I'm just standing there, smelling the fried chicken or the potato salad, and then... well, then I just sort of drift off for a moment. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not very helpful. I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you are in one of these 'stuck' states, if someone were to try to gently move your arm or change your position, would your body feel like it stays exactly where it was placed, like a doll, or would you be able to move normally if they prompted you?\n\nPatient: Oh, my stars, like a little doll? I really hope you don't think I'm... well, losing my mind or anything awful like that. I think my brain just gets a little stuck on a track sometimes, that's all. Just a silly old woman's habit. \n\nDoes that help answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: When you are standing still or repeating those actions, do you feel like you are doing it because you have an irresistible urge or a need to prevent something bad from happening, or does it feel more like your body has simply become stuck and won't move the way you want it to?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no, nothing like that. I don't think anything bad is going to happen at all. I'm not... well, I'm not superstitious or anything, I promise. \n\nAnd it's not really that my body refuses to move, exactly. It's just... I don't rightly know why it happens, to be perfectly honest with you. I just sort of pause, I guess. Like my mind just wanders off for a spell and I just forget to keep going with what my hands are doing. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound too foolish. I'm trying my very best to explain it right for you, but it's just hard to put into words. Did I answer that the way you needed, Doctor? I can try to explain it better if I didn't do a good job.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you ever experience any unusual sensations, like a strange smell, a funny taste in your mouth, or a sudden feeling of déjà vu right before you stop moving?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, sir. Nothing like that. No funny smells or... or tastes, or feeling like I've been there before. \n\nI know some folks get those sorts of things, but I'm just a plain old lady, I promise. Everything feels just perfectly normal right before it happens. I'm just standing there, smelling the fried chicken or the potato salad, and then... well, then I just sort of drift off for a moment. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not very helpful. I know you're trying to figure out what's wrong with me, and I feel awful that I can't give you a better clue. Am I doing alright, Doctor Smith? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you are in one of these 'stuck' states, if someone were to try to gently move your arm or change your position, would your body feel like it stays exactly where it was placed, like a doll, or would you be able to move normally if they prompted you?\n\nPatient: Oh, my stars, like a little doll? No, Doctor Smith, I don't believe so. I think... well, if someone were to touch my arm or call my name, I just snap right back to myself and move just fine. \n\nI certainly wouldn't just stay stuck wherever they put me. Oh, I'd be so embarrassed if someone had to move me around like a mannequin! I'm pretty sure I just go right back to whatever chore I was doing the second someone prompts me. \n\nIs that... is that a good thing? I hope that helps you. I just wouldn't want you to think I'm completely helpless or making a big fuss for the folks around me. Am I doing okay explaining this for you? I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: When you 'snap back' to yourself, do you have any gaps in your memory of what happened while you were standing still, or do you remember everything that was going on around you the whole time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, I... well, I don't think I lose time or anything scary like that. I'm pretty sure I remember everything just fine. \n\nI mean, I'm just standing there, so there's not a whole lot to remember, right? But I can still hear the television or the birds outside, and I know exactly where I am. It's just a little daydream, really, nothing serious at all. I certainly wouldn't want you to think my memory is going bad on me, Doctor. \n\nI try to keep my mind as sharp as a tack! Did that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound too confusing. I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: During these moments, have you ever noticed any twitching or jerking movements in your hands, face, or anywhere else in your body?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor Smith, nothing like that at all. No twitching or... or shaking or anything unsightly. \n\nI'm just perfectly still, like I said. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was having some kind of a fit or making a scene in front of people! I'm always very careful to be polite and proper. It's just a quiet little pause, that's all. \n\nI hope that's the right answer. I mean, I'm telling you the truth, I just... I hope it's what you need to hear to figure this all out. Am I being helpful enough, Doctor? I really am trying my best for you.\n\nDoctor: When you have these moments of drifting off, have you been feeling particularly overwhelmed, stressed, or worried about anything in your life lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no. I... well, I really don't think so. I don't have anything to be all that stressed about, Doctor. My life is very quiet these days. I just putter around the house and mind my own business. \n\nI certainly wouldn't want to complain or make a fuss when there are folks out there with real problems, you know? I just try to count my blessings every day. So, no, no big worries or... or feeling overwhelmed. I handle things just fine, I promise. \n\nI hope that's an alright answer? I'd hate for you to think I'm holding anything back. I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you and giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes lately, such as being a bit more clumsy, having trouble finding the right words, or feeling like your thinking is just a little slower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, well... I certainly wouldn't say I'm clumsy! I'm always very careful on my feet, I make sure of it. But... well, maybe just a little bit of trouble with my words sometimes. Nothing terrible, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, when the ladies ask me about things, like the charity blankets or choir practice, I really do mean to answer them straight. I try to be so polite and proper. But lately, my words just sort of run off on me. I'll start out fine, and then somehow I'm talking about canning green beans, and then the hymns, and then my roses out front... and folks just look at me like they can't quite find what I'm saying. \n\nIt makes me feel a bit silly, to be honest, having them stare at me like that. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish, Doctor! It's just a little conversational detour, really. Nothing to worry too much over, right? Am I explaining that okay for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you are at home doing your usual chores, like cooking or keeping your house tidy, have you noticed that you are finding it harder to finish tasks or follow the steps required to complete them?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all! I keep my house very neat, I really do. I wouldn't want anyone dropping by and thinking I don't take pride in my home. \n\nI mean... well, sometimes I might start folding the laundry and then I'll remember I need to water the African violets, so I go do that, and then... well, maybe the laundry sits for just a little bit. But I always get to it! I'm not leaving things in a mess or anything like that. I just... I like to take my time and do things my own way, that's all. \n\nI still cook my own meals and keep the dust off the knick-knacks just fine. I hope that's what you were asking? I wouldn't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself properly. I'm doing a good job with my chores, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with your memory lately, such as forgetting where you put your keys, missing appointments, or losing track of recent conversations?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. And I remember my conversations just fine, especially with the ladies at church. \n\nSo, really, no trouble with my memory at all. I hope that's a relief to hear? I'd hate for you to worry that my mind is going. I'm keeping everything straight, I assure you. Did I answer that the way you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your handwriting, or does it feel like your hands are a bit stiffer or less steady when you are writing out your to-do lists or notes?\n\nPatient: Oh, my handwriting? No, no, I've always taken a lot of pride in my penmanship. They taught us proper cursive back in my day, you know, and I make sure to keep it very neat. \n\nMy hands... well, maybe they get just a tiny bit stiff on a cold morning, but really, who isn't a little stiff at my age? It's nothing to fuss over, I promise! It certainly doesn't make my writing messy or unsteady. I write out my grocery lists and my little thank-you cards for the church just fine. I'd be so embarrassed if folks couldn't read my notes, so I make sure to take my time and do it right. \n\nSo, no trouble there at all. I hope that's a good sign, Doctor? I'm trying my best to give you all the right details. Does that sound alright to you?\n\nDoctor: When you are talking to your friends, have they ever mentioned that your face looks a bit expressionless, or that you don't seem to blink as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. My friends haven't said anything like that to me. I'd be quite upset if they thought I was just... sitting there like a bump on a log! \n\nI've always been a very warm person, you know? I smile when I greet people, and I nod along when they tell me their stories. I mean, maybe sometimes I get a little tired and don't make a big fuss over every little thing, but I certainly have plenty of expression! And my blinking? Well, I can't say I've ever paid attention to how much I blink, but nobody has ever told me I'm staring at them. That would be terribly rude, wouldn't it? \n\nI always try to be polite and pleasant when I'm visiting. I hope that's what you were looking to hear? I really do try my best to be a good listener for my friends. Is that the sort of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Have any of your friends or family members ever mentioned that you seem to walk a little differently, perhaps with shorter steps or a slight shuffle, or that your arms don't swing as much as they used to when you are walking?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. Nobody has ever said anything like that to me. I've always been quite spry on my feet, you know? \n\nI mean, I suppose I don't go running any marathons these days, and maybe I take my time a little more when I'm walking down to the mailbox, just to be careful not to trip. And my arms... well, I usually have my purse over one arm, or I'm holding onto my little sweater, so maybe they don't swing wildly about, but I certainly walk just like everyone else. I'd be mortified if people thought I was shuffling around like some sort of... invalid! \n\nI try to keep myself looking nice and proper when I'm out and about. I hope that puts your mind at ease, Doctor? I really am doing wonderfully. Is that the kind of answer you were hoping for?\n\nDoctor: When you are planning a more complicated task, like preparing a meal with several different dishes or organizing a small gathering, do you find that it takes you significantly longer to get everything ready than it did in the past?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. Am I doing okay with all these questions?\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any medical conditions by a doctor in the past, or are you currently taking any medications for your health?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no. Oh, goodness, no. I don't think so. I mean... I always try to be polite and proper, of course, especially when I have company or if I'm at the market. It's just good manners, isn't it?
